Therefore, it is in the interest of every individual Austrian that we have the highest possible rate of vaccination and, by means of the so-called herd protection, also those persons who cannot be vaccinated for health or other reasons. But what does the situation look like in Austria? – Well, unfortunately, we do not know exactly because we have no systematic detection, which vaccinations are administered and which are not. We know, however, that vaccination rates in Austria are getting worse and the Austrian population is getting worse and worse. In order to identify the problems and take the right measures, we need first and foremost valid figures. To get these numbers, we will introduce an electronic vaccination pass. We have dealt with this request in the Committee on Health last week, and I hope he will also be able to get your approval here today. But now we come to the vaccination itself. Provocatively formulated, we have an excellent vaccination plan, but we lack a clear strategy as to how we implement it. The free vaccination program for children and infants works especially in combination with the mother-child-pass. Our students are also systematically vaccinated by the school medical service, and we also note that the participation rates are falling. The last structured measure we take in the area of vaccination concerns our ground defenses; the vaccination status is raised by the ground defense service and the lack of vaccinations are supplemented. In principle, the family doctor would be the appointed body for the implementation of the vaccination plan from this point on, but this would be unnecessarily hampered by inefficient parallel structures and uncoordinated individual measures by the authorities and the health insurance companies. In my opinion, in order for the Austrian vaccination plan to be implemented as best as possible in the current constellation, three things are needed: firstly, a clear definition of who and which place is responsible for which vaccination, and especially in the established area, an appropriate remuneration for this activity. Secondly, there is a need for uniform subsidies and costs from the health insurance companies, which is also part of the health insurance reform that we call for. Thirdly, a consistent and sustainable education and information of the Austrian population on the meaning and benefits of the vaccination is necessary. We have also agreed on this point in the government programme. We will try at all levels to improve the health education of Austrians. Mr Loacker's request that pharmacies take over the health service vaccination in the future is evidence of the high confidence in the skills and competence of Austrian pharmacies. [[Abg. Neubauer: Well, that was yesterday!]] Article 16: “Married women and men have the right to marry and to establish a family without limitation on the grounds of race, nationality or religion. They have equal rights at marriage, during marriage, and when they are dissolved.” Ladies and gentlemen! If you look at the milestones of LGBT equalisation policy in Austria, it is very interesting to look at them more closely, namely: In 2002, the Constitutional Court abolished the last special criminal offence – as it was about the age of protection – and in 2013 the ban on step-child adoption, which was lifted by the European Court of Human Rights, in 2014, the VfGH abolished the legal restriction on the medically supported reproduction, and in 2017, the VfGH established the opening of marriage and the opening of the partnership. It is interesting that it has always been courts that have contributed to better equality in Austria. Now the VfGH has announced that it will also examine intersexuality, third gender, and I assume that the VfGH will also remove the restrictions on this issue. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, it will be time for policy to take decisions in the future when it comes to equality in Austria. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and list fungus.]] Ladies and gentlemen! One thing that really surprised me today, namely Mr Jeitler's speech. The fact that the new Austrian gay couple just came from the ÖVP and the FPÖ really deserved an applause. [[with your hands to indicate a condolences]] [[Congratulations with representatives of the SPÖ.]] [[Calls: The reproach won't get better the second time! Fasching's already over.]] If short and straight are already in a marriage, then finally let all Austrians get married!
